     1→
     2→# Agent Implementation Guide
     3→
     4→## Architecture Overview
     5→
     6→The Entire CLI uses **inversion of control** for agent integration: agents are passive data providers that translate their native hook payloads into normalized lifecycle events, and the framework handles all orchestration (state transitions, file detection, checkpoint saving, metadata generation). The flow is:
     7→
     8→```
     9→Agent hook invocation → ParseHookEvent() → Event → DispatchLifecycleEvent() → framework actions
    10→```
    11→
    12→An agent never calls strategy methods or manages session state directly. It only answers questions: "What event just happened?" and "What does the transcript say?"
    13→
    14→## Quick Reference
    15→
    16→### Core Interface (`Agent`)
    17→
    18→Every agent must implement all 19 methods on the `Agent` interface:
    19→
    20→| Group | Method | Purpose |
    21→|-------|--------|---------|
    22→| **Identity** | `Name()` | Registry key (e.g., `"claude-code"`) |
    23→| | `Type()` | Display name for metadata (e.g., `"Claude Code"`) |
    24→| | `Description()` | Human-readable description for UI |
    25→| | `DetectPresence()` | Check if agent is configured in the repo |
    26→| | `ProtectedDirs()` | Directories to preserve during rewind |
    27→| **Event Mapping** | `HookNames()` | Hook verbs that become CLI subcommands |
    28→| | `ParseHookEvent()` | **Core contribution surface** - translate native hooks to Events |
    29→| **Transcript** | `ReadTranscript()` | Read raw transcript bytes |
    30→| | `ChunkTranscript()` | Split large transcripts at format-aware boundaries |
    31→| | `ReassembleTranscript()` | Recombine chunks into a single transcript |
    32→| **Session Management** | `GetHookConfigPath()` | Path to hook config file |
    33→| | `SupportsHooks()` | Whether agent supports lifecycle hooks |
    34→| | `ParseHookInput()` | Parse hook callback input from stdin |
    35→| | `GetSessionID()` | Extract session ID from hook input |
    36→| | `GetSessionDir()` | Where agent stores session data |
    37→| | `ResolveSessionFile()` | Path to session transcript file |
    38→| | `ReadSession()` | Read session data from agent's storage |
    39→| | `WriteSession()` | Write session data for resumption |
    40→| | `FormatResumeCommand()` | Command to resume a session |
    41→
    42→### Optional Interfaces
    43→
    44→| Interface | Methods | When to implement |
    45→|-----------|---------|-------------------|
    46→| `HookSupport` | `InstallHooks`, `UninstallHooks`, `AreHooksInstalled`, `GetSupportedHooks` | Agent uses a config file for hook registration (e.g., `settings.json`) |
    47→| `HookHandler` | `GetHookNames` | **Required for CLI hook registration** — `entire hooks <agent> <verb>` subcommands are only created for agents implementing this interface. Typically delegates to `HookNames()`. |
    48→| `TranscriptAnalyzer` | `GetTranscriptPosition`, `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset`, `ExtractPrompts`, `ExtractSummary` | You want richer checkpoints with transcript-derived file lists and prompts |
    49→| `TranscriptPreparer` | `PrepareTranscript` | Agent writes transcripts asynchronously and needs a flush/sync step |
    50→| `TokenCalculator` | `CalculateTokenUsage` | Agent's transcript contains token usage data |
    51→| `SubagentAwareExtractor` | `ExtractAllModifiedFiles`, `CalculateTotalTokenUsage` | Agent spawns subagents (like Claude Code's Task tool) |
    52→| `FileWatcher` | `GetWatchPaths`, `OnFileChange` | Agent doesn't support hooks; uses file-based detection instead |
    53→
    54→## Step-by-Step Implementation Guide
    55→
    56→### Step 1: Create Package
    57→
    58→Create a new directory under `cmd/entire/cli/agent/`:
    59→
    60→```
    61→cmd/entire/cli/agent/youragent/
    62→├── youragent.go          # Core Agent implementation + init()
    63→├── lifecycle.go          # ParseHookEvent + compile-time assertions
    64→├── types.go              # Hook input structs, transcript types, tool constants
    65→├── hooks.go              # HookSupport implementation (if applicable)
    66→├── transcript.go         # TranscriptAnalyzer implementation (if applicable)
    67→├── lifecycle_test.go     # Tests for ParseHookEvent
    68→├── hooks_test.go         # Tests for hook installation
    69→└── transcript_test.go    # Tests for transcript analysis
    70→```
    71→
    72→### Step 2: Define Types (`types.go`)
    73→
    74→Define structs matching your agent's native hook JSON payloads:
    75→
    76→```go
    77→package youragent
    78→
    79→// Settings file structure (for HookSupport)
    80→type YourAgentSettings struct {
    81→    Hooks YourAgentHooks `json:"hooks"`
    82→}
    83→
    84→type YourAgentHooks struct {
    85→    SessionStart []HookMatcher `json:"SessionStart,omitempty"`
    86→    SessionEnd   []HookMatcher `json:"SessionEnd,omitempty"`
    87→    // ... other hook types your agent supports
    88→}
    89→
    90→type HookMatcher struct {
    91→    Matcher string      `json:"matcher,omitempty"`
    92→    Hooks   []HookEntry `json:"hooks"`
    93→}
    94→
    95→type HookEntry struct {
    96→    Type    string `json:"type"`
    97→    Command string `json:"command"`
    98→}
    99→
   100→// Hook input structs - match your agent's JSON payloads
   101→
   102→type sessionInfoRaw struct {
   103→    SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
   104→    TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
   105→}
   106→
   107→type promptInputRaw struct {
   108→    SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
   109→    TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
   110→    Prompt         string `json:"prompt"`
   111→}
   112→
   113→// Tool constants - tools in your agent that modify files
   114→const (
   115→    ToolWrite = "write_file"
   116→    ToolEdit  = "edit_file"
   117→)
   118→
   119→var FileModificationTools = []string{ToolWrite, ToolEdit}
   120→```
   121→
   122→### Step 3: Implement Core Agent Interface (`youragent.go`)
   123→
   124→```go
   125→package youragent
   126→
   127→import (
   128→    "errors"
   129→    "fmt"
   130→    "io"
   131→    "os"
   132→    "path/filepath"
   133→
   134→    "github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
   135→    "github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
   136→)
   137→
   138→//nolint:gochecknoinits // Agent self-registration is the intended pattern
   139→func init() {
   140→    agent.Register("your-agent", NewYourAgent)
   141→}
   142→
   143→type YourAgent struct{}
   144→
   145→func NewYourAgent() agent.Agent {
   146→    return &YourAgent{}
   147→}
   148→
   149→// --- Identity ---
   150→
   151→func (a *YourAgent) Name() agent.AgentName    { return "your-agent" }
   152→func (a *YourAgent) Type() agent.AgentType     { return "Your Agent" }
   153→func (a *YourAgent) Description() string       { return "Your Agent - description here" }
   154→func (a *YourAgent) ProtectedDirs() []string   { return []string{".youragent"} }
   155→
   156→func (a *YourAgent) DetectPresence() (bool, error) {
   157→    repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   158→    if err != nil {
   159→        repoRoot = "."
   160→    }
   161→    _, err = os.Stat(filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".youragent"))
   162→    if err == nil {
   163→        return true, nil
   164→    }
   165→    return false, nil
   166→}
   167→
   168→// --- Transcript Storage ---
   169→
   170→func (a *YourAgent) ReadTranscript(sessionRef string) ([]byte, error) {
   171→    data, err := os.ReadFile(sessionRef)
   172→    if err != nil {
   173→        return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
   174→    }
   175→    return data, nil
   176→}
   177→
   178→func (a *YourAgent) ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error) {
   179→    // Use JSONL chunking for line-based formats
   180→    return agent.ChunkJSONL(content, maxSize)
   181→    // Or implement format-specific chunking (see geminicli for JSON example)
   182→}
   183→
   184→func (a *YourAgent) ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error) {
   185→    return agent.ReassembleJSONL(chunks), nil
   186→}
   187→
   188→// --- Session Management ---
   189→
   190→func (a *YourAgent) GetHookConfigPath() string                     { return ".youragent/settings.json" }
   191→func (a *YourAgent) SupportsHooks() bool                           { return true }
   192→func (a *YourAgent) GetSessionID(input *agent.HookInput) string    { return input.SessionID }
   193→func (a *YourAgent) FormatResumeCommand(sessionID string) string   { return "youragent --resume " + sessionID }
   194→
   195→// ParseHookInput is part of the Agent interface and is called by integration tests.
   196→// Provide a real implementation that populates at least SessionID and SessionRef.
   197→func (a *YourAgent) ParseHookInput(hookType agent.HookType, r io.Reader) (*agent.HookInput, error) {
   198→    raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](r)
   199→    if err != nil {
   200→        return nil, err
   201→    }
   202→    return &agent.HookInput{
   203→        SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   204→        SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   205→    }, nil
   206→}
   207→
   208→func (a *YourAgent) GetSessionDir(_ string) (string, error) {
   209→    return "", errors.New("not implemented")
   210→}
   211→
   212→func (a *YourAgent) ResolveSessionFile(sessionDir, agentSessionID string) string {
   213→    return filepath.Join(sessionDir, agentSessionID+".jsonl")
   214→}
   215→
   216→func (a *YourAgent) ReadSession(_ *agent.HookInput) (*agent.AgentSession, error) {
   217→    return nil, errors.New("not implemented")
   218→}
   219→
   220→func (a *YourAgent) WriteSession(_ *agent.AgentSession) error {
   221→    return errors.New("not implemented")
   222→}
   223→```
   224→
   225→### Step 4: Implement `ParseHookEvent` (`lifecycle.go`)
   226→
   227→This is the **main contribution surface** for new agents. Map each of your agent's native hook names to the normalized `EventType`:
   228→
   229→```go
   230→package youragent
   231→
   232→import (
   233→    "io"
   234→    "time"
   235→
   236→    "github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
   237→)
   238→
   239→// Hook name constants - these become CLI subcommands
   240→const (
   241→    HookNameSessionStart = "session-start"
   242→    HookNameSessionEnd   = "session-end"
   243→    HookNamePromptSubmit = "prompt-submit"
   244→    HookNameResponse     = "response"
   245→)
   246→
   247→func (a *YourAgent) HookNames() []string {
   248→    return []string{
   249→        HookNameSessionStart,
   250→        HookNameSessionEnd,
   251→        HookNamePromptSubmit,
   252→        HookNameResponse,
   253→    }
   254→}
   255→
   256→func (a *YourAgent) ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*agent.Event, error) {
   257→    switch hookName {
   258→    case HookNameSessionStart:
   259→        raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
   260→        if err != nil {
   261→            return nil, err
   262→        }
   263→        return &agent.Event{
   264→            Type:       agent.SessionStart,
   265→            SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   266→            SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   267→            Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   268→        }, nil
   269→
   270→    case HookNamePromptSubmit:
   271→        raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[promptInputRaw](stdin)
   272→        if err != nil {
   273→            return nil, err
   274→        }
   275→        return &agent.Event{
   276→            Type:       agent.TurnStart,
   277→            SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   278→            SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   279→            Prompt:     raw.Prompt,
   280→            Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   281→        }, nil
   282→
   283→    case HookNameResponse:
   284→        raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
   285→        if err != nil {
   286→            return nil, err
   287→        }
   288→        return &agent.Event{
   289→            Type:       agent.TurnEnd,
   290→            SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   291→            SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   292→            Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   293→        }, nil
   294→
   295→    case HookNameSessionEnd:
   296→        raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
   297→        if err != nil {
   298→            return nil, err
   299→        }
   300→        return &agent.Event{
   301→            Type:       agent.SessionEnd,
   302→            SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   303→            SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   304→            Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   305→        }, nil
   306→
   307→    default:
   308→        // Unknown hooks have no lifecycle significance
   309→        return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil
   310→    }
   311→}
   312→```
   313→
   314→Key decisions in `ParseHookEvent`:
   315→
   316→- **Return `nil, nil`** for hooks with no lifecycle significance (pass-through hooks). This is not an error - it tells the framework to do nothing.
   317→- **Every event must include the required fields for its `EventType`** (see the [Event Field Requirements](#event-field-requirements) table). `SessionID` is always required; `SessionRef` is required for most event types but optional for `Compaction` and `SessionEnd`.
   318→- **`TurnStart` should include `Prompt`** if available - it's used for commit message generation.
   319→- **Use `agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[T]`** - the generic helper reads stdin and unmarshals JSON in one step.
   320→- **Set `Timestamp` to `time.Now()`** - the framework uses this for ordering.
   321→
   322→### Step 5: Choose and Implement Optional Interfaces
   323→
   324→See the [Optional Interface Decision Tree](#optional-interface-decision-tree) section below.
   325→
   326→### Step 6: Register via `init()`
   327→
   328→Registration happens in `init()` in your main agent file. The import side-effect pattern ensures your agent is available when the CLI starts:
   329→
   330→```go
   331→//nolint:gochecknoinits // Agent self-registration is the intended pattern
   332→func init() {
   333→    agent.Register("your-agent", NewYourAgent)
   334→}
   335→```
   336→
   337→Then add a blank import in the CLI's command setup to ensure the package is loaded:
   338→
   339→```go
   340→// In cmd/entire/cli/commands/ or wherever agents are imported
   341→import (
   342→    _ "github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/youragent"
   343→)
   344→```
   345→
   346→### Step 7: Add Compile-Time Interface Assertions
   347→
   348→In `lifecycle.go` (or whichever file implements the optional interfaces), add compile-time checks:
   349→
   350→```go
   351→// Compile-time interface assertions
   352→var (
   353→    _ agent.TranscriptAnalyzer = (*YourAgent)(nil)
   354→    _ agent.TokenCalculator    = (*YourAgent)(nil)
   355→    // Add one line per optional interface you implement
   356→)
   357→```
   358→
   359→Also add assertions for `HookSupport` and `HookHandler` in `hooks.go` if applicable:
   360→
   361→```go
   362→var (
   363→    _ agent.HookSupport = (*YourAgent)(nil)
   364→    _ agent.HookHandler = (*YourAgent)(nil)
   365→)
   366→```
   367→
   368→### Step 8: Implement Hook Installation (if `HookSupport`)
   369→
   370→If your agent uses a JSON config file for hooks (like Claude Code's `.claude/settings.json` or Gemini's `.gemini/settings.json`), implement `HookSupport`:
   371→
   372→```go
   373→func (a *YourAgent) InstallHooks(localDev bool, force bool) (int, error) {
   374→    // 1. Find repo root
   375→    repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   376→    if err != nil {
   377→        return 0, err
   378→    }
   379→
   380→    // 2. Read existing settings (preserve unknown fields)
   381→    settingsPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".youragent", "settings.json")
   382→    // ... read and parse ...
   383→
   384→    // 3. Build hook commands
   385→    // localDev mode uses an agent-specific env var (e.g., ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR},
   386→    // ${GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR}) that the agent expands at runtime. Choose a name
   387→    // for your agent and add it to entireHookPrefixes so existing hooks can be
   388→    // detected/removed during install/uninstall.
   389→    var cmdPrefix string
   390→    if localDev {
   391→        cmdPrefix = "go run ${YOUR_AGENT_PROJECT_DIR}/cmd/entire/main.go hooks your-agent "
   392→    } else {
   393→        cmdPrefix = "entire hooks your-agent "
   394→    }
   395→
   396→    // 4. Add hooks if they don't exist (idempotent)
   397→    // 5. Write settings back (preserving unknown fields)
   398→
   399→    return count, nil
   400→}
   401→
   402→func (a *YourAgent) UninstallHooks() error         { /* reverse of install */ }
   403→func (a *YourAgent) AreHooksInstalled() bool        { /* check settings file */ }
   404→func (a *YourAgent) GetSupportedHooks() []agent.HookType { /* list supported types */ }
   405→```
   406→
   407→Also implement `HookHandler` — this is required for the CLI to register `entire hooks <agent> <verb>` subcommands:
   408→
   409→```go
   410→func (a *YourAgent) GetHookNames() []string {
   411→    return a.HookNames() // delegate to the core interface method
   412→}
   413→```
   414→
   415→### Step 9: Write Tests
   416→
   417→Test `ParseHookEvent` for every hook name your agent supports. See [Testing Patterns](#testing-patterns) below.
   418→
   419→## Event Mapping Reference
   420→
   421→The framework dispatcher (`DispatchLifecycleEvent` in `lifecycle.go`) handles each event type as follows:
   422→
   423→| Event Type | Framework Actions | Claude Code Hook | Gemini CLI Hook |
   424→|------------|-------------------|------------------|-----------------|
   425→| `SessionStart` | Shows banner, checks concurrent sessions, fires state machine transition | `session-start` | `session-start` |
   426→| `TurnStart` | Captures pre-prompt state (git status, transcript position), ensures strategy setup, initializes session | `user-prompt-submit` | `before-agent` |
   427→| `TurnEnd` | Validates transcript, extracts metadata (prompts, summary, files), detects file changes via git status, saves step + checkpoint, transitions phase to IDLE | `stop` | `after-agent` |
   428→| `Compaction` | Fires compaction transition (stays ACTIVE), resets transcript offset | *(not used)* | `pre-compress` |
   429→| `SessionEnd` | Marks session as ENDED in state machine | `session-end` | `session-end` |
   430→| `SubagentStart` | Captures pre-task state (git status snapshot) | `pre-task` (PreToolUse[Task]) | *(not used)* |
   431→| `SubagentEnd` | Extracts subagent modified files, detects changes, saves task checkpoint | `post-task` (PostToolUse[Task]) | *(not used)* |
   432→
   433→### Event Field Requirements
   434→
   435→| Event Type | Required Fields | Optional Fields |
   436→|------------|----------------|-----------------|
   437→| `SessionStart` | `SessionID`, `SessionRef` | `ResponseMessage` |
   438→| `TurnStart` | `SessionID`, `SessionRef` | `Prompt`, `PreviousSessionID` |
   439→| `TurnEnd` | `SessionID`, `SessionRef` | |
   440→| `Compaction` | `SessionID` | `SessionRef` |
   441→| `SessionEnd` | `SessionID` | `SessionRef` |
   442→| `SubagentStart` | `SessionID`, `SessionRef`, `ToolUseID` | `ToolInput` |
   443→| `SubagentEnd` | `SessionID`, `SessionRef`, `ToolUseID` | `SubagentID`, `ToolInput` |
   444→
   445→## Optional Interface Decision Tree
   446→
   447→### `TranscriptAnalyzer`
   448→
   449→**What it enables:** Transcript-derived file lists (more accurate than git-status-only), extracted user prompts in checkpoint metadata, session summaries.
   450→
   451→**Without it:** The framework still creates checkpoints using git-status-based file detection and stores the raw transcript. Prompts and summary fields will be empty.
   452→
   453→**Implement when:** Your agent writes a parseable transcript (JSONL, JSON, or any structured format) and you can extract which files were modified and what the user asked.
   454→
   455→**Methods:**
   456→- `GetTranscriptPosition(path) (int, error)` - Return current position. For JSONL: line count. For JSON with messages array: message count.
   457→- `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(path, startOffset) (files, currentPosition, error)` - Parse transcript from offset and return files touched by write/edit tools.
   458→- `ExtractPrompts(sessionRef, fromOffset) ([]string, error)` - Extract user prompt strings.
   459→- `ExtractSummary(sessionRef) (string, error)` - Extract last assistant response as summary.
   460→
   461→### `TranscriptPreparer`
   462→
   463→**What it enables:** A pre-read synchronization step before the framework reads the transcript.
   464→
   465→**Without it:** The framework reads the transcript immediately, which may be incomplete if the agent writes asynchronously.
   466→
   467→**Implement when:** Your agent writes transcripts asynchronously (e.g., Claude Code uses an async writer and needs to wait for a flush sentinel before reading).
   468→
   469→**Method:**
   470→- `PrepareTranscript(sessionRef) error` - Wait until the transcript is fully written. Called before `ReadTranscript`.
   471→
   472→### `TokenCalculator`
   473→
   474→**What it enables:** Token usage metrics in checkpoint metadata (input, output, cache tokens, API call count).
   475→
   476→**Without it:** Token usage fields are empty in checkpoint metadata.
   477→
   478→**Implement when:** Your agent's transcript contains token/usage data per message.
   479→
   480→**Method:**
   481→- `CalculateTokenUsage(sessionRef, fromOffset) (*TokenUsage, error)` - Sum token usage from offset to end of transcript.
   482→
   483→### `SubagentAwareExtractor`
   484→
   485→**What it enables:** Includes files modified by spawned subagents in the checkpoint file list and aggregates subagent token usage.
   486→
   487→**Without it:** Only the main agent's transcript is analyzed. Subagent modifications are still captured by git status but not attributed to the subagent.
   488→
   489→**Implement when:** Your agent spawns subagents (task workers) that have their own transcripts.
   490→
   491→**Methods:**
   492→- `ExtractAllModifiedFiles(sessionRef, fromOffset, subagentsDir) ([]string, error)` - Deduplicated file list from main + subagent transcripts.
   493→- `CalculateTotalTokenUsage(sessionRef, fromOffset, subagentsDir) (*TokenUsage, error)` - Aggregated usage including subagents.
   494→
   495→### `HookSupport`
   496→
   497→**What it enables:** `entire enable` automatically installs hooks into the agent's config file.
   498→
   499→**Without it:** Users must manually configure hooks to call `entire hooks <agent> <verb>`.
   500→
   501→**Implement when:** Your agent supports a config file with hook definitions (e.g., `.claude/settings.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`).
   502→
   503→### `FileWatcher`
   504→
   505→**What it enables:** Detecting session activity by watching file changes instead of hooks.
   506→
   507→**Without it:** The agent must support hooks for the framework to receive events.
   508→
   509→**Implement when:** Your agent doesn't support lifecycle hooks but writes session data to predictable file paths.
   510→
   511→## Transcript Format Guide
   512→
   513→### JSONL Format (Claude Code pattern)
   514→
   515→One JSON object per line. Each line is a transcript entry (user message, assistant message, tool use, etc.):
   516→
   517→```
   518→{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Fix the bug"},"timestamp":"..."}
   519→{"type":"assistant","message":{"role":"assistant","content":[...]},"timestamp":"..."}
   520→```
   521→
   522→**Chunking:** Use `agent.ChunkJSONL(content, maxSize)` - splits at newline boundaries.
   523→**Reassembly:** Use `agent.ReassembleJSONL(chunks)` - concatenates with newlines.
   524→**Position:** Line count (`bufio.Reader` + count `\n`).
   525→**Offset:** Start parsing at line N (skip first N lines).
   526→
   527→### JSON Format (Gemini CLI pattern)
   528→
   529→Single JSON object with a `messages` array:
   530→
   531→```json
   532→{"messages": [{"type": "user", "content": "..."}, {"type": "gemini", "content": "..."}]}
   533→```
   534→
   535→**Chunking:** Parse the JSON, split the messages array across chunks, marshal each chunk as a complete JSON object with a subset of messages.
   536→**Reassembly:** Parse each chunk, concatenate all message arrays, marshal back.
   537→**Position:** Message count (`len(transcript.Messages)`).
   538→**Offset:** Start iterating messages at index N.
   539→
   540→### Using Chunking Helpers
   541→
   542→The `agent` package provides format-agnostic entry points:
   543→
   544→```go
   545→// These dispatch to the agent's ChunkTranscript/ReassembleTranscript methods
   546→agent.ChunkTranscript(content, agentType)     // agentType → agent lookup → format-aware chunking
   547→agent.ReassembleTranscript(chunks, agentType)  // agentType → agent lookup → format-aware reassembly
   548→
   549→// Direct JSONL helpers (usable without an agent)
   550→agent.ChunkJSONL(content, maxSize)
   551→agent.ReassembleJSONL(chunks)
   552→
   553→// Chunk file naming
   554→agent.ChunkFileName("full.jsonl", 0)  // "full.jsonl"
   555→agent.ChunkFileName("full.jsonl", 1)  // "full.jsonl.001"
   556→agent.ParseChunkIndex("full.jsonl.002", "full.jsonl")  // 2
   557→agent.SortChunkFiles(files, "full.jsonl")  // sorted by chunk index
   558→```
   559→
   560→## Hook Installation Patterns
   561→
   562→### JSON Config File Pattern
   563→
   564→Both Claude Code and Gemini CLI use a JSON settings file in their config directory. The installation pattern is:
   565→
   566→1. **Read existing settings** as `map[string]json.RawMessage` to preserve unknown fields
   567→2. **Parse only the hook types you modify** into typed slices
   568→3. **Remove existing Entire hooks** (for `force` mode or mode-switching)
   569→4. **Add new hooks** idempotently (check if command already exists)
   570→5. **Marshal modified types back** to the raw map
   571→6. **Write the file** with pretty-printing
   572→
   573→Key principles:
   574→- **Preserve unknown fields** - don't destroy user's custom hooks or settings
   575→- **Idempotent installs** - running `entire enable` twice doesn't duplicate hooks
   576→- **Support `localDev` mode** - use `go run ${PROJECT_DIR}/...` for development
   577→- **Identify Entire hooks** by command prefix (e.g., `"entire "` or `"go run ${...}"`)
   578→
   579→### Example: Claude Code Hook Config
   580→
   581→```json
   582→{
   583→  "hooks": {
   584→    "SessionStart": [{"matcher": "", "hooks": [{"type": "command", "command": "entire hooks claude-code session-start"}]}],
   585→    "Stop": [{"matcher": "", "hooks": [{"type": "command", "command": "entire hooks claude-code stop"}]}],
   586→    "PreToolUse": [{"matcher": "Task", "hooks": [{"type": "command", "command": "entire hooks claude-code pre-task"}]}]
   587→  }
   588→}
   589→```
   590→
   591→### Example: Gemini CLI Hook Config
   592→
   593→```json
   594→{
   595→  "hooksConfig": {"enabled": true},
   596→  "hooks": {
   597→    "SessionStart": [{"hooks": [{"name": "entire-session-start", "type": "command", "command": "entire hooks gemini session-start"}]}],
   598→    "AfterAgent": [{"hooks": [{"name": "entire-after-agent", "type": "command", "command": "entire hooks gemini after-agent"}]}]
   599→  }
   600→}
   601→```
   602→
   603→Note: Gemini CLI requires `hooksConfig.enabled: true` and each hook entry requires a `name` field.
   604→
   605→## Testing Patterns
   606→
   607→### Testing `ParseHookEvent`
   608→
   609→Test every hook name, including pass-through hooks that return nil:
   610→
   611→```go
   612→func TestParseHookEvent_TurnStart(t *testing.T) {
   613→    t.Parallel()
   614→
   615→    ag := &YourAgent{}
   616→    input := `{"session_id": "sess-123", "transcript_path": "/tmp/t.jsonl", "prompt": "Fix the bug"}`
   617→
   618→    event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(HookNamePromptSubmit, strings.NewReader(input))
   619→
   620→    if err != nil {
   621→        t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   622→    }
   623→    if event == nil {
   624→        t.Fatal("expected event, got nil")
   625→    }
   626→    if event.Type != agent.TurnStart {
   627→        t.Errorf("expected TurnStart, got %v", event.Type)
   628→    }
   629→    if event.Prompt != "Fix the bug" {
   630→        t.Errorf("expected prompt 'Fix the bug', got %q", event.Prompt)
   631→    }
   632→}
   633→```
   634→
   635→### Testing Nil Returns
   636→
   637→Hooks with no lifecycle action must return `nil, nil`:
   638→
   639→```go
   640→func TestParseHookEvent_PassThrough_ReturnsNil(t *testing.T) {
   641→    t.Parallel()
   642→
   643→    ag := &YourAgent{}
   644→    event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent("some-pass-through-hook", strings.NewReader(`{}`))
   645→
   646→    if err != nil {
   647→        t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   648→    }
   649→    if event != nil {
   650→        t.Errorf("expected nil event, got %+v", event)
   651→    }
   652→}
   653→```
   654→
   655→### Testing Error Cases
   656→
   657→```go
   658→func TestParseHookEvent_EmptyInput(t *testing.T) {
   659→    t.Parallel()
   660→
   661→    ag := &YourAgent{}
   662→    _, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(HookNameSessionStart, strings.NewReader(""))
   663→
   664→    if err == nil {
   665→        t.Fatal("expected error for empty input")
   666→    }
   667→    if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "empty hook input") {
   668→        t.Errorf("expected 'empty hook input' error, got: %v", err)
   669→    }
   670→}
   671→
   672→func TestParseHookEvent_MalformedJSON(t *testing.T) {
   673→    t.Parallel()
   674→
   675→    ag := &YourAgent{}
   676→    _, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(HookNameSessionStart, strings.NewReader("not json"))
   677→
   678→    if err == nil {
   679→        t.Fatal("expected error for malformed JSON")
   680→    }
   681→}
   682→```
   683→
   684→### Testing Hook Installation
   685→
   686→```go
   687→func TestInstallHooks_CreatesSettingsFile(t *testing.T) {
   688→    t.Parallel()
   689→
   690→    dir := t.TempDir()
   691→    // ... set up test repo, create .youragent/ directory ...
   692→
   693→    ag := &YourAgent{}
   694→    count, err := ag.InstallHooks(false, false)
   695→
   696→    if err != nil {
   697→        t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   698→    }
   699→    if count == 0 {
   700→        t.Error("expected hooks to be installed")
   701→    }
   702→
   703→    // Verify settings file was created and contains hooks
   704→    data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(dir, ".youragent", "settings.json"))
   705→    // ... assert hooks are present ...
   706→}
   707→
   708→func TestInstallHooks_Idempotent(t *testing.T) {
   709→    t.Parallel()
   710→
   711→    // Install twice, second call should return count=0
   712→    ag := &YourAgent{}
   713→    ag.InstallHooks(false, false)
   714→    count, _ := ag.InstallHooks(false, false)
   715→    if count != 0 {
   716→        t.Errorf("expected 0 new hooks on second install, got %d", count)
   717→    }
   718→}
   719→```

   730→## Common Pitfalls
   731→
   732→### go-git v5 Bugs
   733→
   734→**Do NOT use go-git v5 for `checkout` or `reset --hard` operations.** go-git v5 has a bug where `worktree.Reset()` with `HardReset` and `worktree.Checkout()` incorrectly delete untracked directories even when listed in `.gitignore`. This would destroy `.entire/` and agent config directories. Use the git CLI instead. See `CLAUDE.md` for details and `hard_reset_test.go` for regression tests.
   735→
   736→### Repo Root vs Current Working Directory
   737→
   738→Git commands return paths relative to the **repository root**, not the current working directory. When your code runs from a subdirectory, `os.Getwd()` gives wrong results for path construction. Always use `paths.RepoRoot()`:
   739→
   740→```go
   741→// WRONG
   742→cwd, _ := os.Getwd()
   743→absPath := filepath.Join(cwd, file) // Breaks from subdirectory
   744→
   745→// CORRECT
   746→repoRoot, _ := paths.RepoRoot()
   747→absPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, file)
   748→```
   749→
   750→### Transcript Flush Timing
   751→
   752→Some agents write transcripts asynchronously. If `ReadTranscript` is called before the write completes, the transcript will be incomplete. Implement `TranscriptPreparer` if your agent has this behavior. Claude Code solves this by writing a sentinel entry and polling for it (see `waitForTranscriptFlush` in `claudecode/lifecycle.go`).
   753→
   754→### Nil Event Return Pattern
   755→
   756→`ParseHookEvent` returning `(nil, nil)` is **not an error** - it means the hook has no lifecycle significance. The framework (in `hook_registry.go`) checks:
   757→
   758→```go
   759→event, parseErr := ag.ParseHookEvent(hookName, stdin)
   760→if event != nil {
   761→    hookErr = DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag, event)
   762→}
   763→// nil event → no-op (hook is silently acknowledged)
   764→```
   765→
   766→Use `//nolint:nilnil` to suppress the linter warning on intentional nil returns.
   767→
   768→### Agent Name vs Agent Type
   769→
   770→- `AgentName` is the **registry key** used in code (`"claude-code"`, `"gemini"`). It appears in CLI commands: `entire hooks claude-code stop`.
   771→- `AgentType` is the **display name** stored in metadata and commit trailers (`"Claude Code"`, `"Gemini CLI"`). It's what users see.
   772→
   773→Register constants for both in `cmd/entire/cli/agent/registry.go` when adding a new agent.
   774→
   775→### Hook Names as CLI Subcommands
   776→
   777→The strings returned by `HookNames()` become literal CLI subcommands under `entire hooks <agent>`. For example, if `HookNames()` returns `["session-start", "stop"]`, the CLI creates:
   778→- `entire hooks your-agent session-start`
   779→- `entire hooks your-agent stop`
   780→
   781→These commands read JSON from stdin and dispatch to `ParseHookEvent`. The agent's hook config should invoke these commands.
   782→
   783→## Complete Code Template
   784→
   785→A minimal but functional agent skeleton. Copy this directory structure and fill in agent-specific details:
   786→
   787→<details>
   788→<summary>youragent/types.go</summary>
   789→
   790→```go
   791→package youragent
   792→
   793→// sessionInfoRaw matches your agent's session hook JSON payload.
   794→type sessionInfoRaw struct {
   795→	SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
   796→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
   797→}
   798→
   799→// promptInputRaw matches your agent's prompt-submit hook JSON payload.
   800→type promptInputRaw struct {
   801→	SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
   802→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
   803→	Prompt         string `json:"prompt"`
   804→}
   805→```
   806→
   807→</details>
   808→
   809→<details>
   810→<summary>youragent/youragent.go</summary>
   811→
   812→```go
   813→package youragent
   814→
   815→import (
   816→	"errors"
   817→	"fmt"
   818→	"io"
   819→	"os"
   820→	"path/filepath"
   821→
   822→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
   823→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths"
   824→)
   825→
   826→//nolint:gochecknoinits // Agent self-registration is the intended pattern
   827→func init() {
   828→	agent.Register("your-agent", NewYourAgent)
   829→}
   830→
   831→type YourAgent struct{}
   832→
   833→func NewYourAgent() agent.Agent { return &YourAgent{} }
   834→
   835→func (a *YourAgent) Name() agent.AgentName  { return "your-agent" }
   836→func (a *YourAgent) Type() agent.AgentType   { return "Your Agent" }
   837→func (a *YourAgent) Description() string     { return "Your Agent - brief description" }
   838→func (a *YourAgent) ProtectedDirs() []string { return []string{".youragent"} }
   839→
   840→func (a *YourAgent) DetectPresence() (bool, error) {
   841→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   842→	if err != nil {
   843→		repoRoot = "."
   844→	}
   845→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".youragent")); err == nil {
   846→		return true, nil
   847→	}
   848→	return false, nil
   849→}
   850→
   851→func (a *YourAgent) ReadTranscript(sessionRef string) ([]byte, error) {
   852→	data, err := os.ReadFile(sessionRef) //nolint:gosec // Path from agent hook
   853→	if err != nil {
   854→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
   855→	}
   856→	return data, nil
   857→}
   858→
   859→func (a *YourAgent) ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error) {
   860→	return agent.ChunkJSONL(content, maxSize)
   861→}
   862→
   863→func (a *YourAgent) ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error) {
   864→	return agent.ReassembleJSONL(chunks), nil
   865→}
   866→
   867→// --- Session Management ---
   868→func (a *YourAgent) GetHookConfigPath() string                                          { return "" }
   869→func (a *YourAgent) SupportsHooks() bool                                                { return true }
   870→func (a *YourAgent) ParseHookInput(_ agent.HookType, r io.Reader) (*agent.HookInput, error) {
   871→	raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](r)
   872→	if err != nil {
   873→		return nil, err
   874→	}
   875→	return &agent.HookInput{SessionID: raw.SessionID, SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath}, nil
   876→}
   877→func (a *YourAgent) GetSessionID(input *agent.HookInput) string                         { return input.SessionID }
   878→func (a *YourAgent) GetSessionDir(_ string) (string, error)                             { return "", errors.New("not implemented") }
   879→func (a *YourAgent) ResolveSessionFile(dir, id string) string                           { return filepath.Join(dir, id+".jsonl") }
   880→func (a *YourAgent) ReadSession(_ *agent.HookInput) (*agent.AgentSession, error)        { return nil, errors.New("not implemented") }
   881→func (a *YourAgent) WriteSession(_ *agent.AgentSession) error                           { return errors.New("not implemented") }
   882→func (a *YourAgent) FormatResumeCommand(id string) string                               { return "youragent --resume " + id }
   883→```
   884→
   885→</details>
   886→
   887→<details>
   888→<summary>youragent/lifecycle.go</summary>
   889→
   890→```go
   891→package youragent
   892→
   893→import (
   894→	"io"
   895→	"time"
   896→
   897→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
   898→)
   899→
   900→const (
   901→	HookNameSessionStart = "session-start"
   902→	HookNameSessionEnd   = "session-end"
   903→	HookNamePromptSubmit = "prompt-submit"
   904→	HookNameResponse     = "response"
   905→)
   906→
   907→func (a *YourAgent) HookNames() []string {
   908→	return []string{
   909→		HookNameSessionStart,
   910→		HookNameSessionEnd,
   911→		HookNamePromptSubmit,
   912→		HookNameResponse,
   913→	}
   914→}
   915→
   916→func (a *YourAgent) ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*agent.Event, error) {
   917→	switch hookName {
   918→	case HookNameSessionStart:
   919→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
   920→		if err != nil {
   921→			return nil, err
   922→		}
   923→		return &agent.Event{
   924→			Type:       agent.SessionStart,
   925→			SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   926→			SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   927→			Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   928→		}, nil
   929→
   930→	case HookNamePromptSubmit:
   931→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[promptInputRaw](stdin)
   932→		if err != nil {
   933→			return nil, err
   934→		}
   935→		return &agent.Event{
   936→			Type:       agent.TurnStart,
   937→			SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   938→			SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   939→			Prompt:     raw.Prompt,
   940→			Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   941→		}, nil
   942→
   943→	case HookNameResponse:
   944→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
   945→		if err != nil {
   946→			return nil, err
   947→		}
   948→		return &agent.Event{
   949→			Type:       agent.TurnEnd,
   950→			SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   951→			SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   952→			Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   953→		}, nil
   954→
   955→	case HookNameSessionEnd:
   956→		raw, err := agent.ReadAndParseHookInput[sessionInfoRaw](stdin)
   957→		if err != nil {
   958→			return nil, err
   959→		}
   960→		return &agent.Event{
   961→			Type:       agent.SessionEnd,
   962→			SessionID:  raw.SessionID,
   963→			SessionRef: raw.TranscriptPath,
   964→			Timestamp:  time.Now(),
   965→		}, nil
   966→
   967→	default:
   968→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil event = no lifecycle action
   969→	}
   970→}
   971→```
   972→
   973→</details>

   979→package youragent
   980→
   981→import (
   982→	"strings"
   983→	"testing"
   984→
   985→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
   986→)
   987→
   988→func TestParseHookEvent_SessionStart(t *testing.T) {
   989→	t.Parallel()
   990→
   991→	ag := &YourAgent{}
   992→	input := `{"session_id": "test-session", "transcript_path": "/tmp/transcript.jsonl"}`
   993→
   994→	event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(HookNameSessionStart, strings.NewReader(input))
   995→
   996→	if err != nil {
   997→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   998→	}
   999→	if event == nil {
  1000→		t.Fatal("expected event, got nil")
  1001→	}
  1002→	if event.Type != agent.SessionStart {
  1003→		t.Errorf("expected SessionStart, got %v", event.Type)
  1004→	}
  1005→	if event.SessionID != "test-session" {
  1006→		t.Errorf("expected session_id 'test-session', got %q", event.SessionID)
  1007→	}
  1008→}
  1009→
  1010→func TestParseHookEvent_TurnStart(t *testing.T) {
  1011→	t.Parallel()
  1012→
  1013→	ag := &YourAgent{}
  1014→	input := `{"session_id": "sess-1", "transcript_path": "/tmp/t.jsonl", "prompt": "Hello"}`
  1015→
  1016→	event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(HookNamePromptSubmit, strings.NewReader(input))
  1017→
  1018→	if err != nil {
  1019→		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
  1020→	}
  1021→	if event.Type != agent.TurnStart {
  1022→		t.Errorf("expected TurnStart, got %v", event.Type)
  1023→	}
  1024→	if event.Prompt != "Hello" {
  1025→		t.Errorf("expected prompt 'Hello', got %q", event.Prompt)
  1026→	}
  1027→}
  1028→
  1029→func TestParseHookEvent_TurnEnd(t *testing.T) {
  1030→	t.Parallel()
  1031→
  1032→	ag := &YourAgent{}
  1033→	input := `{"session_id": "sess-2", "transcript_path": "/tmp/t.jsonl"}`
  1034→
  1035→	event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(HookNameResponse, strings.NewReader(input))
  1036→

Perfect! Now I have enough information to provide a comprehensive summary. Let me create a detailed report.

## Agent Integration Architecture - Comprehensive Exploration Report

I've completed a thorough exploration of the codebase. Here's what I found:

---

### 1. AGENTS THAT EXIST

Two agents are currently implemented and registered in the system:

1. **Claude Code** (`claude-code`)
   - Location: `/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/`
   - Type identifier: `Claude Code`
   - Config file: `.claude/settings.json`
   - Protected dirs: `.claude/`
   - Transcript format: JSONL (one JSON object per line)
   - Session storage: `~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-repo-path>/`

2. **Gemini CLI** (`gemini`)
   - Location: `/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/`
   - Type identifier: `Gemini CLI`
   - Config file: `.gemini/settings.json`
   - Protected dirs: `.gemini/`
   - Transcript format: JSON (with messages array)
   - Session storage: `~/.gemini/tmp/<sanitized-repo-path>/chats/`

Both are self-registering via `init()` functions that call `agent.Register()`.

---

### 2. AGENT INTERFACES & EXTENSION POINTS

The agent system is built on a **modular interface pattern**. Core file: `/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go`

**Core Agent Interface** (all agents must implement):
- **Identity methods:**
  - `Name()` → AgentName (registry key)
  - `Type()` → AgentType (display name in metadata)
  - `Description()` → human-readable string
  - `DetectPresence()` → bool (auto-detect agent in repo)
  - `ProtectedDirs()` → []string (directories to never delete)

- **Event mapping:**
  - `HookNames()` → []string (e.g., `["session-start", "stop", "user-prompt-submit"]`)
  - `ParseHookEvent(hookName, stdin)` → `*agent.Event` (normalize agent hooks to lifecycle events)

- **Transcript storage:**
  - `ReadTranscript(sessionRef)` → []byte
  - `ChunkTranscript(content, maxSize)` → [][]byte (format-aware chunking)
  - `ReassembleTranscript(chunks)` → []byte

- **Legacy methods** (being phased out):
  - `GetHookConfigPath()`, `SupportsHooks()`, `ParseHookInput()`, `GetSessionID()`, `GetSessionDir()`, `ResolveSessionFile()`, `ReadSession()`, `WriteSession()`, `FormatResumeCommand()`

**Optional Interfaces** (agents can implement to add features):

1. **HookSupport** - Hook installation/management
   - `InstallHooks(localDev, force)` → int, error
   - `UninstallHooks()` → error
   - `AreHooksInstalled()` → bool
   - `GetSupportedHooks()` → []HookType

2. **TranscriptAnalyzer** - Rich transcript parsing
   - `GetTranscriptPosition(path)` → int (line count or message index)
   - `ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(path, offset)` → []string, int, error
   - `ExtractPrompts(sessionRef, fromOffset)` → []string, error
   - `ExtractSummary(sessionRef)` → string, error

3. **TranscriptPreparer** - Pre-read processing (e.g., flush waiting)
   - `PrepareTranscript(sessionRef)` → error

4. **TokenCalculator** - Token usage extraction
   - `CalculateTokenUsage(sessionRef, fromOffset)` → *TokenUsage, error

5. **SubagentAwareExtractor** - Support for spawned subagents
   - `ExtractAllModifiedFiles(sessionRef, fromOffset, subagentsDir)` → []string, error
   - `CalculateTotalTokenUsage(sessionRef, fromOffset, subagentsDir)` → *TokenUsage, error

6. **FileWatcher** - Polling-based detection (for agents without hooks)
   - `GetWatchPaths()` → []string, error
   - `OnFileChange(path)` → *SessionChange, error

---

### 3. HOOK SYSTEM & LIFECYCLE EVENT DISPATCH

The hook system consists of three layers:

**Layer 1: Agent-Native Hooks** (installed in agent config files like `.claude/settings.json`)
- Claude Code hooks: `session-start`, `session-end`, `stop`, `user-prompt-submit`, `pre-task`, `post-task`, `post-todo`
- Gemini CLI hooks: `session-start`, `session-end`, `before-agent`, `after-agent`, `before-tool`, `after-tool`, `before-model`, `after-model`, `before-tool-selection`, `pre-compress`, `notification`
- Each hook is installed as a command: `entire hooks <agent> <hook-name>`

**Layer 2: Hook Command Registration** (`/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go`)
- Entry point: `newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging(agentName, hookName)`
- Reads stdin, calls `agent.ParseHookEvent(hookName, stdin)`
- Routes to lifecycle dispatcher if event is non-nil
- Special case: `post-todo` handled directly (Claude-specific)

**Layer 3: Lifecycle Event Dispatcher** (`/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go`)
- Function: `DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag, event)` - routes normalized events to handlers
- Supported event types (in `/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/event.go`):
  - `SessionStart` → handleLifecycleSessionStart
  - `TurnStart` (user submits prompt) → handleLifecycleTurnStart
  - `TurnEnd` (agent stops) → handleLifecycleTurnEnd
  - `Compaction` (context window compression) → handleLifecycleCompaction
  - `SessionEnd` → handleLifecycleSessionEnd
  - `SubagentStart` (task spawned) → handleLifecycleSubagentStart
  - `SubagentEnd` (task completed) → handleLifecycleSubagentEnd

**Hook Input Structures:**
- Agents parse native hooks into normalized `agent.Event` structs
- Event contains: `Type`, `SessionID`, `SessionRef`, `Prompt` (for TurnStart), `ToolUseID`, `SubagentID`, `ToolInput`, `Metadata`

**Example: Claude Code Hook Flow**
1. Agent invokes: `entire hooks claude-code user-prompt-submit` with JSON stdin
2. Hook handler reads JSON: `{"session_id": "...", "transcript_path": "...", "prompt": "..."}`
3. `ParseHookEvent()` converts to: `Event{Type: TurnStart, SessionID, SessionRef, Prompt}`
4. `DispatchLifecycleEvent()` routes to `handleLifecycleTurnStart()`
5. Handler saves checkpoint, updates session state, etc.

---

### 4. AGENT DETECTION & REGISTRY

**Registry System** (`/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/registry.go`):
- Factory pattern: `Register(name, Factory)` called from agent `init()` functions
- Thread-safe with `sync.RWMutex`
- Global functions:
  - `Get(name)` → Agent
  - `List()` → []AgentName (sorted)
  - `Detect()` → Agent (first agent whose `DetectPresence()` returns true)
  - `DetectAll()` → []Agent (all detected agents)
  - `GetByAgentType(agentType)` → Agent (lookup by display name)

**Detection Logic:**
- Each agent checks for its config directory in repo root (e.g., `.claude`, `.gemini`)
- Deterministic ordering: always sorts by agent name, so results are reproducible
- Used in: setup command, resume command, hook invocation

---

### 5. HOOK INSTALLATION & CONFIGURATION

**Claude Code Hook Installation** (`/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/hooks.go`):
- Reads/parses existing `.claude/settings.json`
- Adds hook commands under `hooks.<HookType>[].hooks[]`
- Structure:
  ```
  {
    "hooks": {
      "SessionStart": [{"matcher": "", "hooks": [{"type": "command", "command": "entire hooks claude-code session-start"}]}],
      "Stop": [{"matcher": "", "hooks": [...]}],
      "PreToolUse": [{"matcher": "Task", "hooks": [...]}, {"matcher": "TodoWrite", "hooks": [...]}],
      "PostToolUse": [{"matcher": "Task", "hooks": [...]}, {"matcher": "TodoWrite", "hooks": [...]}],
      ...
    },
    "permissions": {
      "deny": ["Read(./.entire/metadata/**)"]  // Blocks Claude from reading Entire metadata
    }
  }
  ```
- Methods:
  - `InstallHooks(localDev, force)` - installs or updates hooks
  - `UninstallHooks()` - removes Entire hooks
  - `AreHooksInstalled()` - checks if installed

**Gemini CLI Hook Installation** (`/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/hooks.go`):
- Not yet fully implemented in the codebase I reviewed
- Structure likely similar to Claude Code

---

### 6. ARCHITECTURE PATTERNS

**Design Principles:**

1. **Agent Abstraction**: Agents are passive data providers; the framework handles orchestration
2. **Native Format Preservation**: Each agent's session data stays in its native format (JSONL for Claude, JSON for Gemini)
3. **Pluggable Interfaces**: Core interface is minimal; optional interfaces allow agents to opt-in to features
4. **Deterministic Ordering**: Agent detection and listing are sorted for reproducible results
5. **Hook-Driven Events**: Agents emit native hooks → normalized events → lifecycle dispatch
6. **Transcript Analyzer Pattern**: Agents can extract structured data (files, prompts, tokens) from transcripts

**Key Files Map:**

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `agent/agent.go` | Core interfaces |
| `agent/registry.go` | Agent factory/discovery |
| `agent/event.go` | Normalized event types |
| `agent/session.go` | Session data structure |
| `agent/types.go` | Hook input types |
| `agent/claudecode/claude.go` | Claude Code agent implementation |
| `agent/claudecode/hooks.go` | Hook installation for Claude |
| `agent/claudecode/lifecycle.go` | ParseHookEvent and transcript analysis |
| `agent/geminicli/gemini.go` | Gemini agent implementation |
| `agent/geminicli/lifecycle.go` | ParseHookEvent for Gemini |
| `hook_registry.go` | Hook command registration |
| `lifecycle.go` | Event dispatcher (routes to handlers) |
| `setup.go` | User-facing setup command |

---

### 7. EXTENDING WITH NEW AGENTS

To add a new agent (e.g., "Factory AI"):

1. **Create agent package**: `/cmd/entire/cli/agent/factoryai/`

2. **Implement core interface**:
   ```go
   type FactoryAIAgent struct{}
   
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) Name() agent.AgentName { return "factory-ai" }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) Type() agent.AgentType { return "Factory AI" }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) Description() string { return "Factory AI Agent" }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) DetectPresence() (bool, error) { /* check for .factory dir */ }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) HookNames() []string { /* return hook list */ }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) ParseHookEvent(hookName, stdin) (*agent.Event, error) { /* convert hooks */ }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) ReadTranscript(sessionRef) ([]byte, error) { /* read native format */ }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) ChunkTranscript(content, maxSize) ([][]byte, error) { /* chunk */ }
   func (f *FactoryAIAgent) ReassembleTranscript(chunks) ([]byte, error) { /* reassemble */ }
   // ... other required methods
   ```

3. **Self-register**: In `factoryai.go`:
   ```go
   func init() {
       agent.Register(agent.AgentName("factory-ai"), NewFactoryAIAgent)
   }
   ```

4. **Implement optional interfaces** as needed:
   - `TranscriptAnalyzer` for extracting files/prompts/tokens
   - `HookSupport` for hook installation
   - `TokenCalculator` for token tracking
   - `SubagentAwareExtractor` for subagent support

5. **Hook parsing**: Implement `ParseHookEvent()` to map native hooks to normalized event types

6. **Transcript format**: Implement chunking/reassembly for your format (JSONL, JSON, custom)

---

### 8. CURRENT FLOW EXAMPLE: Claude Code Session Start

1. User starts Claude Code session
2. Claude invokes registered hook: `entire hooks claude-code session-start`
3. Hook handler (`hook_registry.go`):
   - Reads stdin JSON: `{"session_id": "abc123", "transcript_path": "/home/user/.claude/projects/.../abc123.jsonl"}`
   - Calls `claudecode.ParseHookEvent("session-start", stdin)`
4. ParseHookEvent (`claudecode/lifecycle.go`):
   - Parses JSON into `sessionInfoRaw`
   - Creates `Event{Type: SessionStart, SessionID: "abc123", SessionRef: "...", Timestamp: now}`
   - Returns event
5. Dispatcher (`lifecycle.go`):
   - Routes to `handleLifecycleSessionStart(ag, event)`
   - Shows banner: "Powered by Entire: This conversation will be linked to your next commit"
   - Checks for concurrent sessions
   - Fires state machine transition `EventSessionStart`
6. State machine updates session phase: `IDLE + TurnStart → ACTIVE`
7. Framework continues...

---

This architecture is highly extensible and allows new agents to integrate cleanly without modifying core framework code. Each agent is a self-contained package that implements standard interfaces.

# Factory AI Overview

**What is Factory AI?**

Factory is a platform for agent-native software development that integrates AI agents called "Droids" directly into existing developer workflows. The company positions itself around the concept that these agents should "work everywhere you do" rather than forcing developers to switch tools.

**What are Droids?**

Droids are software development agents designed to handle complete coding tasks like refactors, incident response, and migrations. According to the webpage, they're "the only software development agents that work everywhere you do."

**How They Work**

Droids operate across multiple interfaces without requiring workflow changes:
- **IDE/Terminal Integration**: VS Code, JetBrains, Vim, and other editors
- **Web Interface**: Browser-based agent execution without setup
- **Command Line**: For CI/CD pipelines and scaled operations
- **Slack/Teams**: Team communication integration for incident triage
- **Project Management Tools**: Automatic triggering from issue assignments

The system maintains "full traceability from ticket to code" when integrated with project managers.

**Key Features**

- Model and vendor agnostic architecture
- Enterprise-grade security protocols
- Integration with existing development stacks
- Parallel execution capabilities for large-scale tasks
- Automated code review and self-healing builds

The platform emphasizes maintaining developer autonomy while automating routine and complex coding responsibilities across the entire development lifecycle.

## CLI Reference & Documentation

Factory's CLI supports JSON output formatting for scripting and automation, and the `droid exec` command uses tiered autonomy to control what operations the agent can perform. Droid Exec is Factory's headless CLI mode designed for automation like CI pipelines, cron jobs, pre-commit hooks, and one-off batch scripts.

## Key CLI Features

The droid exec command supports multiple autonomy levels (read-only, low, medium, and high) to control the scope of operations. The `/mcp` slash command opens an interactive manager UI for browsing and managing MCP servers, with 40+ pre-configured servers available including Linear, Sentry, Notion, Stripe, and Vercel.

## Integration & Hooks

Factory.ai integrates with the broader ecosystem of tools including source control, project trackers, and knowledge bases, and supports integration of external APIs or data sources into workflows.

## Platform Integration

Factory Droids embed directly into your workflow across IDE, Web, CLI, Slack, and Linear, and can be scripted and parallelized at massive scale for CI/CD, migrations, and maintenance.

For more detailed API documentation, you may want to visit the official Factory documentation at docs.factory.ai.

# Factory Droid CLI Reference Summary

## CLI Commands

The documentation lists these primary commands:

- **`droid`** - "Start interactive REPL"
- **`droid "query"`** - Initialize REPL with a prompt
- **`droid exec "query"`** - "Execute task without interactive mode"
- **`droid exec -f prompt.md`** - Load instructions from a file
- **`droid exec -s <id> "query"`** - "Resume existing session in exec mode"
- **`droid exec --list-tools`** - Display available tools
- **`droid update`** - Update to the latest version

## Key Flags

Notable options include:

- **`-m, --model <id>`** - Select a specific AI model
- **`--auto <level>`** - "Set autonomy level" (low/medium/high)
- **`--enabled-tools / --disabled-tools`** - Control tool availability
- **`-o, --output-format`** - Choose output as text, JSON, or streaming formats
- **`-r, --reasoning-effort`** - Adjust reasoning intensity
- **`--use-spec`** - "Start in specification mode (plan before executing)"
- **`--cwd <path>`** - Execute from a specific directory

Based on the search results, here's information about Factory AI Droid CLI hooks, lifecycle events, session transcripts, and format configuration:

## Hooks Overview

Droid hooks are user-defined shell commands that execute at various points in Droid's lifecycle, providing deterministic control over Droid's behavior to ensure certain actions always happen rather than relying on Droid to choose to run them.

## Lifecycle Events

Droid provides several hook events including PreToolUse (runs before tool calls and can block them) and SessionEnd (runs when Droid session ends).

## Session Transcript Format

Hook inputs contain common fields including session_id, transcript_path (path to conversation JSON), cwd (current working directory when the hook is invoked), permission_mode (current permission mode: "default", "plan", "acceptEdits", or "bypassPermissions"), and hook_event_name. The transcript_path follows a format like "~/.factory/projects/.../00893aaf-19fa-41d2-8238-13269b9b3ca0.jsonl".

## Hook Output and Configuration

Hooks communicate status through exit codes: exit code 0 indicates success (stdout is shown to the user in transcript mode, except for UserPromptSubmit and SessionStart where stdout is added to context), and exit code 2 indicates a blocking error (stderr is fed back to Droid to process automatically).

## Example Use Cases

Hook use cases include notifications, automatic formatting (running prettier on .ts files after edits), logging for compliance or debugging, providing automated feedback on code conventions, and custom permissions like blocking modifications to production files.

For comprehensive reference documentation, Factory provides detailed hooks reference materials in their official documentation.

# Factory Droid Hook Configuration

## Configuration File Format and Location

Hooks are configured in `~/.factory/settings.json` for user-level settings or project-level configurations. The format uses a nested JSON structure organized by hook event type:

```json
{
  "hooks": {
    "EventType": [
      {
        "matcher": "ToolName",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "shell command here"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

## Available Hook Events

Droid provides nine hook events:

- **PreToolUse**: "Runs before tool calls (can block them)"
- **PostToolUse**: Executes after tool calls complete
- **UserPromptSubmit**: Triggers when users submit prompts, before processing
- **Notification**: Runs when Droid sends notifications
- **Stop**: Executes when Droid finishes responding
- **SubagentStop**: Runs when sub-droid tasks complete
- **PreCompact**: Runs before compact operations
- **SessionStart**: Triggers on session start or resume
- **SessionEnd**: Executes when sessions terminate

## Hook Type

Currently, one hook type exists: **command**, which executes shell commands.

## Input/Output Handling

Hooks receive JSON data via stdin containing tool information and parameters. In the quickstart example, the command `jq -r '.tool_input.command'` extracts specific fields from stdin JSON. Hooks can read this piped JSON data and process it using tools like `jq` to extract relevant information for logging, validation, or modification purposes.

## Critical Path Requirement

"Always use absolute paths when referencing scripts in your hook commands, not relative paths" because hooks execute from Droid's current working directory, not necessarily your project root.
```

# Factory Droid CLI Settings Configuration

## File Location

Settings are stored in a JSON file at platform-specific paths:
- **macOS/Linux:** `~/.factory/settings.json`
- **Windows:** `%USERPROFILE%\.factory\settings.json`

The file is created automatically with defaults on first run.

## Core Configuration Structure

The settings file supports the following primary fields:

**Model & Reasoning:**
- `model` - AI provider selection (opus, sonnet, gpt-5.1, haiku, gemini-3-pro, etc.)
- `reasoningEffort` - Thinking level: off, none, low, medium, high

**Behavior:**
- `autonomyLevel` - Command execution mode (normal, spec, auto-low, auto-medium, auto-high)
- `cloudSessionSync` - Mirror sessions to web (true/false)
- `diffMode` - Display format (github or unified)

**Audio Feedback:**
- `completionSound` - Response finished notification (fx-ok01, fx-ack01, bell, off, or custom path)
- `awaitingInputSound` - User input prompt sound (same options)
- `soundFocusMode` - When to play sounds (always, focused, unfocused)

**Security & Git:**
- `commandAllowlist` - Auto-executable commands (array)
- `commandDenylist` - Always-confirm commands (array)
- `includeCoAuthoredByDroid` - Git commit trailer (true/false)
- `enableDroidShield` - Secret scanning (true/false)

**Advanced Options:**
- `hooksDisabled` - Global hook toggle (true/false)
- `ideAutoConnect` - IDE connection from external terminals (true/false)
- `todoDisplayMode` - Display style (inline or pinned)
- `specSaveEnabled` - Persist spec outputs (true/false)
- `specSaveDir` - Output directory (default: `.factory/docs`)
- `customModels` - BYOK configurations (array)

## Example Configuration

```json
{
  "model": "opus",
  "reasoningEffort": "low",
  "diffMode": "github",
  "cloudSessionSync": true,
  "completionSound": "fx-ok01",
  "awaitingInputSound": "fx-ack01",
  "soundFocusMode": "always",
  "commandAllowlist": ["ls", "pwd"],
  "commandDenylist": ["rm -rf /"]
}
```

**Note:** The documentation provided does not include a complete hooks configuration schema—only references that hooks can be toggled globally via `hooksDisabled`.

# Complete Factory Droid Hook Events Reference

## Hook Events Summary

Factory Droid supports **9 hook event types**. Here's the complete reference:

---

## 1. **PreToolUse**

**When it fires:** After Droid creates tool parameters and before processing the tool call.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `permission_mode` (common)
- `hook_event_name`: "PreToolUse"
- `tool_name`: The tool being invoked
- `tool_input`: Tool-specific parameters

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success, stdout shown in transcript mode
- **Exit 2**: Blocking error—prevents tool execution, stderr fed to Droid
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error, stderr shown to user

**Special behavior:** Supports matchers (regex patterns). Can output JSON with `permissionDecision` ("allow", "deny", "ask") and `updatedInput` to modify tool parameters before execution.

---

## 2. **PostToolUse**

**When it fires:** Immediately after a tool completes successfully.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "PostToolUse"
- `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_response`

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success, stdout shown in transcript
- **Exit 2**: Shows stderr to Droid (tool already executed)
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** Supports same matchers as PreToolUse. JSON output can include `decision: "block"` with `reason` to prompt Droid or `additionalContext`.

---

## 3. **UserPromptSubmit**

**When it fires:** When user submits a prompt, before Droid processes it.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "UserPromptSubmit"
- `prompt`: The user's prompt text

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Allows processing; stdout injected as context (special case)
- **Exit 2**: Blocking error—erases prompt, shows stderr to user only
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** No matchers. JSON output with `decision: "block"` prevents processing. `additionalContext` in JSON adds to Droid's context.

---

## 4. **Notification**

**When it fires:** When Droid sends notifications (permission requests or idle waiting alerts).

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "Notification"
- `message`: Notification text

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success
- **Exit 2**: Shows stderr to user only
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** No matchers. Cannot block notifications.

---

## 5. **Stop**

**When it fires:** When Droid finishes responding (not triggered by user interrupt).

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "Stop"
- `stop_hook_active`: Boolean indicating if hook already triggered continuation

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success
- **Exit 2**: Blocking error—prevents stoppage, stderr fed to Droid
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** No matchers. JSON output with `decision: "block"` requires `reason` string for Droid guidance. Check `stop_hook_active` to prevent infinite loops.

---

## 6. **SubagentStop**

**When it fires:** When a sub-droid (Task tool call) finishes responding.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "SubagentStop"
- `stop_hook_active`: Boolean flag

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success
- **Exit 2**: Blocking error—prevents stoppage, stderr fed to sub-droid
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** No matchers. Functionally similar to Stop but operates at sub-droid level. JSON `decision: "block"` requires `reason`.

---

## 7. **PreCompact**

**When it fires:** Before Droid runs a compact operation.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "PreCompact"
- `trigger`: "manual" (from `/compact`) or "auto" (context window full)
- `custom_instructions`: User instructions (manual only) or empty string

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success
- **Exit 2**: Shows stderr to user only (cannot block)
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** Matchers: `manual` or `auto`.

---

## 8. **SessionStart**

**When it fires:** When Droid starts a new session or resumes an existing session.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "SessionStart"
- `source`: "startup", "resume" (from `--resume`/`--continue`), "clear" (from `/clear`), or "compact"

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success; stdout injected as context
- **Exit 2**: Shows stderr to user only (cannot block)
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** No matchers. JSON output with `additionalContext` loads development context (issues, dependencies). Multiple hooks' contexts concatenate.

---

## 9. **SessionEnd**

**When it fires:** When a Droid session terminates.

**Input fields (stdin JSON):**
- Common fields plus `hook_event_name`: "SessionEnd"
- `reason`: "clear", "new", "logout", "prompt_input_exit", or "other"

**Output/Exit Codes:**
- **Exit 0**: Success
- **Exit 2**: Shows stderr to user only (cannot block)
- **Other codes**: Non-blocking error

**Special behavior:** No matchers. Cannot prevent session termination; useful for cleanup and logging.

---

## Execution Defaults

- **Timeout:** 60 seconds per command (configurable)
- **Parallelization:** Matching hooks run in parallel
- **Environment:** Includes `FACTORY_PROJECT_DIR` (project root) and `DROID_PLUGIN_ROOT` (plugin directory)
- **Deduplication:** Identical commands automatically deduplicated
```

# Session Management in Droid Exec

Based on the documentation provided, information about session management specifics is limited. Here's what is documented:

## Session Continuation

The CLI supports resuming previous work through the `--session-id` parameter:

> "Existing session to continue (requires a prompt)"

You can continue a session with:
```bash
droid exec --session-id <session-id> "continue with next steps"
```

## Session Identification

Sessions are tracked via unique identifiers that appear in command output. When using JSON format, the response includes:

> "Extract session IDs for continuation"

The session ID is returned in structured output formats and can be used to pick up work later.

## Notable Gaps

The documentation does **not** specify:
- Physical storage location of session files
- Transcript format or structure
- Session persistence mechanisms
- Retention policies
- Local vs. cloud storage details

For comprehensive information on session storage architecture and transcript formats, you would need to consult additional Factory documentation or contact support directly.

# SpecStory & Factory Droid Integration

## Integration Method

SpecStory captures Factory Droid CLI sessions through the command `specstory run droid`. As documented, users can "keep using Droid's features like spec mode planning, skills, and slash commands like `/review`" while SpecStory records the interaction.

## Transcript Format & Storage

Sessions are converted to Markdown files stored in `.specstory/history/` with timestamps and metadata included. This allows conversations and reasoning to remain "intact as Markdown in your repo."

## Session Tracking

SpecStory provides three mechanisms for capturing Droid activity:

1. **Active Launch**: `specstory run droid` starts a monitored session
2. **Retroactive Sync**: `specstory sync droid` converts previously-created sessions from `~/.factory/` (Droid's local storage)
3. **Continuous Watch**: `specstory watch droid` auto-saves new activity to markdown without requiring explicit startup

The system reads from Factory Droid's session database and exports complete conversation histories with full context preservation, enabling easy sharing and archival alongside codebases.

# How droid-sync Accesses Factory Droid Sessions

## Access Method

The plugin receives session data through **subprocess hooks**. When Factory Droid triggers an event, it "Invokes `droid-sync hook <EventName>` as a subprocess" and "Pipes JSON context to stdin (sessionId, transcriptPath, cwd, etc.)."

## Transcript Location and Format

The transcript path is passed via stdin JSON from Factory Droid. The documentation states the plugin performs "JSONL transcript parsing, incremental message extraction" but doesn't specify the exact filesystem location where Factory Droid stores transcripts.

## Transcript Structure

The transcript uses **JSONL format** (JSON Lines—one JSON object per line). The plugin:

1. Parses the JSONL file on each Stop event
2. Extracts messages incrementally
3. Tracks synced message IDs to avoid duplicates

The documentation shows the plugin transforms Factory Droid's internal fields:
- `messageId` → backend's `externalId`
- `content` → `textContent`
- `tool_use` blocks → structured `parts` array

## Key Limitation

The README doesn't provide the actual JSONL schema structure or absolute file paths where Factory Droid stores transcripts—only that the transcript path comes from the hook's stdin JSON context.

Based on the web search results, I found information about Factory AI Droid CLI configuration:

## Project-Level Settings

The Factory CLI uses a ~/.factory/ folder structure that includes settings.json for CLI settings and allow/deny lists. For project-specific configuration, custom droids can be enabled in Factory by adding "enableCustomDroids": true to ~/.factory/settings.json.

## .factory Directory Structure

The ~/.factory/ directory contains: config.json (model configuration and BYOK), settings.json (CLI settings and allow/deny lists), mcp.json (MCP integrations), droids/ (custom sub-agents), commands/ (custom slash commands), logs/ (session logs), and bug-reports/ (crash reports).

## Project-Level Custom Configuration

Custom droids live as .md files under either your project's .factory/droids/ or your personal ~/.factory/droids/ directory. Project droids sit in <repo>/.factory/droids/ and are shared with teammates. Personal droids live in ~/.factory/droids/ and follow you across workspaces.

For specifications and documentation, Droid saves approved specs as markdown files in .factory/docs/, and you can toggle this on or off and specify the save directory in the settings using the /settings command.

The settings.json files support configuration for command allowlists/denylists, custom models, and other CLI-level preferences that can be layered between user-level and project-level configurations.

     5→// ClaudeSettings represents the .claude/settings.json structure
     6→type ClaudeSettings struct {
     7→	Hooks ClaudeHooks `json:"hooks"`
     8→}
     9→
    10→// ClaudeHooks contains the hook configurations
    11→type ClaudeHooks struct {
    12→	SessionStart     []ClaudeHookMatcher `json:"SessionStart,omitempty"`
    13→	SessionEnd       []ClaudeHookMatcher `json:"SessionEnd,omitempty"`
    14→	UserPromptSubmit []ClaudeHookMatcher `json:"UserPromptSubmit,omitempty"`
    15→	Stop             []ClaudeHookMatcher `json:"Stop,omitempty"`
    16→	PreToolUse       []ClaudeHookMatcher `json:"PreToolUse,omitempty"`
    17→	PostToolUse      []ClaudeHookMatcher `json:"PostToolUse,omitempty"`
    18→}
    19→
    20→// ClaudeHookMatcher matches hooks to specific patterns
    21→type ClaudeHookMatcher struct {
    22→	Matcher string            `json:"matcher"`
    23→	Hooks   []ClaudeHookEntry `json:"hooks"`
    24→}
    25→
    26→// ClaudeHookEntry represents a single hook command
    27→type ClaudeHookEntry struct {
    28→	Type    string `json:"type"`
    29→	Command string `json:"command"`
    30→}
    31→
    32→// sessionInfoRaw is the JSON structure from SessionStart/SessionEnd/Stop hooks
    33→type sessionInfoRaw struct {
    34→	SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
    35→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
    36→}
    37→
    38→// userPromptSubmitRaw is the JSON structure from UserPromptSubmit hooks.
    39→// Unlike other session hooks, this includes the user's prompt text.
    40→type userPromptSubmitRaw struct {
    41→	SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
    42→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path"`
    43→	Prompt         string `json:"prompt"`
    44→}
    45→

    19→// Compile-time interface assertions for new interfaces.
    20→var (
    21→	_ agent.TranscriptAnalyzer     = (*ClaudeCodeAgent)(nil)
    22→	_ agent.TranscriptPreparer     = (*ClaudeCodeAgent)(nil)
    23→	_ agent.TokenCalculator        = (*ClaudeCodeAgent)(nil)
    24→	_ agent.SubagentAwareExtractor = (*ClaudeCodeAgent)(nil)
